The agreement and facts modules identify these maps with the knot interpolation and prove downwardness, the contact values, and the unary inequality. The final dependency hierarchy is     

> theorem_4_10 ←(degree12Atomwise_proved, degree12UnaryStatement), degree12Atomwise_proved ←(degree12Plus_proved, complementedOffSwitch_proved).

The statement quantifies over independent occurrence slots. Repeated-variable cases follow by identifying slots, without any change to the formal theorem. 

C.3 Analytical architecture of the formal proof 

A switching face consists of points where the two arguments of an atom roof agree. A strict knot configuration is a point off that face with every occurrence slot equal to a knot. On a knot box and an affine roof branch, a deficit is affine in each occurrence slot separately. Moving each slot to an endpoint reduces a minimum off the switching face to a strict knot configuration or to the face itself. Repeated coordinates are imposed only after this reduction on the larger independent-slot domain. Two analytical estimates reduce the cases on switching faces. First, for 𝑋, 𝑌 ≥ 0, 

𝑋 + 𝑌 = max   

> 0≤𝜃 ≤1

𝑋 𝜃 𝑌 1− 𝜃 

𝜃 𝜃 (1 − 𝜃 )1− 𝜃 ,

with the continuous endpoint conventions; when 𝑋 + 𝑌 > 0, the maximizing weight is 𝜃 = 𝑋 /( 𝑋 + 𝑌 ). This is the weighted AM–GM variational identity. Second, let 𝑘 ≥ 1, let 𝑥 1, . . . , 𝑥 𝑘 lie in an interval on which 0 < 𝑟 0 ≤ 𝑟 1, and set 𝑃 𝜈 = Î𝑘 𝑖 =1 𝑟 𝜈 (𝑥 𝑖 ) and ¯𝑥 = 𝑘 −1 Í𝑖 𝑥 𝑖 < 1. Let 𝐶 0, 𝐶 1 ≥ 0. If 𝐶 0 𝑃 0 + 𝐶 1 𝑃 1 = 0, the bound below is immediate. Otherwise define its normalized first-product weight, called the posterior weight , by 𝜃 = 𝐶 0 𝑃 0/( 𝐶 0 𝑃 0 + 𝐶 1 𝑃 1). Suppose 0 ≤ 𝜃 0 ≤ 𝜃 ≤ 1 and 

𝜃 0 log 𝑟 0 (𝑥 𝑖 ) + ( 1 − 𝜃 0) log 𝑟 1 (𝑥 𝑖 ) ≤ log (1 − ¯𝑥 ) − 𝑥 𝑖 − ¯𝑥 

1 − ¯𝑥 

61 Concave roofs and downward recovery Donglei Du 

for each 𝑖 . Summation at 𝜃 and the variational identity give 

𝐶 0 𝑃 0 + 𝐶 1 𝑃 1 ≤ ( 𝐶 0 + 𝐶 1)( 1 − ¯𝑥 )𝑘 .

We call this estimate posterior–tangent compression. It replaces the head values by a bound involving their average, reducing the remaining cases to equal heads or to one distinguished head with the others equal. The proof separates the two polarities and, for each, points on and off the switching face. On complemented switching faces, a log-ratio estimate between 1 − 𝛼 12 and 1 − 𝛽 12 bounds the posterior weight. Replacing several heads by their common average then leaves bounds for one distinguished head. Complemented off-switch cases reach the switching face by monotonicity or scaling; other cases are bounded directly or extended to rank eleven by adding zero heads. On positive switching faces, explicit product bounds handle ranks four through eleven, with separate calculations for smaller ranks. Off the positive switching face, the endpoint reduction and exact product bounds at knots suffice, except that rank two requires separate quadratic calculations. The final assembly modules are 

> RoofRecovery/Degree12/ComplementedOffSwitchCompleted.lean RoofRecovery/Degree12/PositiveOffSwitchCompleted.lean

The finite data are rational. Algebraic residuals use exact arithmetic, interval monotonicity, and rational Bernstein coefficients; logarithmic and exponential residuals use analytic inequalities and rational-power bounds. No inequality is accepted on the basis of a floating-point test. For 𝑚 ≥ 5, both maps are the identity at 1 /( 2𝑚 ). At root 𝑎 = 1/2 and equal complemented heads 

𝑢 𝑖 = 1/( 2𝑚 ), the necessary inequality for this portfolio is 1 − ( 1 − 1/( 2𝑚 )) 𝑚 ≥ 2/5. Exact values at the terminal ranks are 1 − ( 21 /22 )11 − 2/5 = 1567401522879 2921591507056640 > 0,

1 − ( 23 /24 )12 − 2/5 = − 12079851931877 182601737180282880 < 0.

The listed portfolio therefore reaches literal degree twelve and fails at thirteen. Theorem 8.4 gives the stronger conclusion that even the supremum over all finite common-coordinatewise termwise portfolio factors is strictly below 8 /17 at degree thirteen. This analytical proof is independent of the degree-twelve formalization. 

C.4 Reproduction and verification scope 

Use the commit specified above without updating the dependency manifest. From its repository root, the documented commands are 

lake exe cache get LEAN_NUM_THREADS=4 lake build lake env lean RoofRecovery/Degree12/Theorem410AxiomAudit.lean 

The final command prints the axiom dependencies of the exported theorem and its principal assembled com-ponents. The repository records the kernel-checked proof and its axiom audit. The statement correspondence above matches the exported theorem to Theorem 5.5. 

# D Exact-arithmetic verification for arity-four common maps 

To prove Theorem 6.3, it suffices to verify inequalities on a complete set of cone generators over the marginal cube. The companion supplies exact certificates for the affine majorants, coverage of the domain, and polynomial nonnegativity. 62 Concave roofs and downward recovery Donglei Du 

D.1 Polyhedral and roof reductions 

The cone of nonnegative submodular value tables on four labeled elements has the 16 inequalities 𝑔 (𝑆 ) ≥ 0and the 24 elementary submodularity inequalities 

𝑔 (𝐾 ∪ { 𝑖 }) + 𝑔 (𝐾 ∪ { 𝑗 }) − 𝑔 (𝐾 ) − 𝑔 (𝐾 ∪ { 𝑖, 𝑗 }) ≥ 0, 𝑖 < 𝑗, 𝐾 ⊆ [ 4] \ { 𝑖, 𝑗 }.

The exact double-description enumeration gives 222 primitive integer ray generators, partitioned by coordinate permutations into 21 orbits. Double description adds the defining inequalities one at a time, updating the complete list of extreme rays after each addition. Each primitive generator is normalized to greatest common divisor one. Fixed common maps are invariant under these permutations. Since 𝐺 𝑔 = Í𝑟 𝜆 𝑟 𝐺 𝑟 and 

𝑔 + ≤ Í𝑟 𝜆 𝑟 𝑟 + for nonnegative conic coefficients, it suffices to verify the same weighted map inequality on each ray. For a ray 𝑟 , an affine majorant ℓ satisfies ℓ(1𝑆 ) ≥ 𝑟 (𝑆 ) at all 16 Boolean points. Taking expectations gives 

ℓ(𝑥 ) ≥ 𝑟 + (𝑥 ). The minimum of a collection of valid majorants is also an upper bound. It therefore suffices to prove the recovery inequality against a selected affine majorant or a convex combination of valid majorants. For the single map 𝜙 ∗, partition at 1 /4 and 2 /3. The active region of a stored majorant ℓ is the set where ℓ

is no larger than any other stored majorant. Intersect each knot box with these regions. Triangulate each region and express 𝐺 𝑟 (𝜙 ∗ (𝑥 )) − 𝛾 ∗ℓ(𝑥 ) in a Bernstein basis. Nonnegative coefficients prove nonnegativity on the simplex. The certificate records 3,461 active cells, 72,540 initial simplices, and 75,123 final simplices, after at most five exact longest-edge bisections. Every accepted sign is decided exactly in the field 

Q(√3) = {𝑎 + 𝑏 √3 : 𝑎, 𝑏 ∈ Q}; floating-point tolerances play no role. For the rational pair 𝜓 0, 𝜓 1, each coordinate has 22 knot intervals. For a fixed affine majorant, the polynomial 

𝐻 𝑟 ,ℓ (𝑥 ) = 8𝐺 𝑟 (𝜓 0 (𝑥 )) + 9𝐺 𝑟 (𝜓 1 (𝑥 )) − 8ℓ(𝑥 )

is affine in each coordinate separately. Nonnegative values at all 16 corners prove nonnegativity throughout the box. The same argument applies to an exact convex combination of two or three majorants. The 21 representatives give 21 · 22 4 = 4,919 ,376 coarse boxes. The certificate records 6,276 adaptive nodes with maximum depth eight. When the deficit vanishes along a boundary, the corner test may fail to certify a box. The companion calls these singular cells . Partition each such box into chambers indexed by a minimizing majorant. In the chamber for 𝑗 , the inequalities ℓ𝑗 ≤ ℓ𝑘 hold for every active 𝑘 . Each factor ℎ is either a nonnegative affine box slack, such as 𝑥 𝑖 − 𝑙 𝑖 or 𝑢 𝑖 − 𝑥 𝑖 , or an ordering slack ℓ𝑘 − ℓ𝑗 .A Handelman identity is such a nonnegative linear combination of products of affine slacks. # E Representation freedom and multilinear sharpness 

Changing a representation allows coefficient adjustments as well as root reallocations. The adjustments that can change the roof form a fractional triple-packing system. Triangle-free capacity graphs force this packing to be zero, preserving the roofs of the sharpness examples. 

E.1 All uniform-polarity representation freedom in degree three 

Let 𝐹 (𝑥 ) = Í|𝑆 | ≤ 3 𝑎 𝑆 𝑥 𝑆 be nonnegative and submodular on the Boolean cube. For a pair 𝑒 , put

# F Concave-cardinality and symmetric sequences 

An explicit decomposition of concave sequences into affine terms and tents reduces the proof to these generators. Pairwise averaging then reduces recovery to binomial expectations, from which the sharp constants and witnesses follow. 

F.1 The full nonnegative concave sequence cone 

For 𝑑 ≥ 2, let ℎ(0), . . . , ℎ (𝑑 ) be concave and nonnegative. Define

# G Exact overlap examples and portfolio separation 

Two overlap examples establish bounds relative to additive local roofs. We then show that the 23-knot portfolios fail each other’s fixed-weight inequalities, despite sharing knots and contact values.

72 Concave roofs and downward recovery Donglei Du 

For 𝜌 < 0, each component of ∇𝐻 𝐺 is nonincreasing in both profile coordinates. To bound a rectangle, the verifier evaluates 𝐻 𝐺 at its center and integrates the endpoint derivative enclosures along each of the two coordinate orders. Both give valid variation bounds, so it retains the smaller one. A Gaussian quantile is Φ−1 (𝑎 ). MPFR provides multiple-precision floating-point arithmetic with specified rounding modes; binary64 uses a 53-bit significand. The verifier uses round-to-nearest binary64 arithmetic and is compiled without unsafe reassociation or fast-math options. Gaussian quantiles are located by 220 bisection steps at 256-bit precision. The integral is evaluated with MPFR arithmetic, directed output rounding, the analytic error (H.4), and an outward 10 −12 allowance at every density node. The derivative enclosures use a further 10 −9 outward allowance for conversion of quantiles and Gaussian arguments to binary64 before the final MPFR evaluation of the normal cumulative distribution function (CDF). The verifier records these allowances explicitly. The bounds below show that they cover conversion and roundoff errors throughout the allowed subdivision depth and correlation interval. At subdivision depth at most 40, every interior profile coordinate passed to the quantile routine lies in 

[2−41 , 1 − 2−41 ], so its normal quantile lies in [− 8, 8]. The 220-step MPFR bisection and final binary64 conversion give absolute quantile error below 4 × 10 −15 . If 𝑔 denotes the density in (H.3), completing the square gives, uniformly for 𝑟 ∈ [− 151 /200 , 0],

|𝜕 𝑥 𝑔 |, |𝜕 𝑦 𝑔 | ≤ 𝑒 −1/2

2𝜋𝑑 0

< 0.225 .

The 10 −12 density allowance therefore covers quantile conversion and binary64 errors in the base term. The round-to-nearest operations internal to each 256-bit MPFR density evaluation, including the stored values of 

𝜋 and the quadrature node, contribute less than 10 −70 in absolute value: on the stated domain all algebraic intermediates are bounded, the denominator is at least 2 𝜋 √𝑑 0, and fewer than 40 correctly rounded operations are performed. The same precision makes CDF roundoff negligible relative to the quantile-conversion bound. The verifier stores the right-hand side of (H.4) in binary64; it is below the exact rational remainder by less than 2 × 10 −24 . The 10 −12 node allowance contributes at least 

|𝜌 𝐵𝐵 |10 −12 = 933 9067 10 −12 > 10 −13 

to every computed Gaussian integral, and therefore covers the stored remainder’s deficit and the density-evaluation errors above. Using binary64 unit roundoff 2 −53 , |𝑥 |, |𝑦 | ≤ 8, and √︁ 1 − 𝜌 2 ≥ √𝑑 0 > 0.65, direct propagation through each derivative argument gives error below 10 −12 . Since sup 𝑧 Φ′ (𝑧 ) = 1/√2𝜋 < 0.4and the CDF truncation beyond 12 is below 2 × 10 −33 , the 10 −9 derivative allowance is conservative. 

H.3 Certificate output 

A cell’s pruning margin is the binary64 comparison target minus the computed upper bound on 𝐻 𝐺 over the entire cell. The rounding reserve is an additional margin that covers errors in the final assembly of this bound and rounding of the comparison target. A cell is pruned , and becomes a terminal cell , when its margin is finite and exceeds the reserve; it is then not subdivided further. The reported residual margin is the smallest pruning margin minus the reserve, rounded downward. Version gaussian-reserve-v1 uses this acceptance rule and reports: 73 Concave roofs and downward recovery Donglei Du 

precision 256 bits trapezoidal panels 4,000 stored quadrature addend 3.5864072916666665 × 10 −8

cells visited 595 cells pruned 298 maximum subdivision depth 23 largest point upper bound 0.058212883635230132 location of largest point bound (0.44775390625 , 0.0712890625 )

smallest final pruning margin 8.4277705231228239 × 10 −9

required final reserve 2.0000000000000004 × 10 −12 

residual margin lower bound 8.4257705231228217 × 10 −9

The verifier’s binary64 comparison target is 0 .058213000000000001, which exceeds the exact rational 58213 /1000000 by less than 9 × 10 −19 . The smallest reported pruning margin is greater than 8 .42 × 10 −9.We allow a further 10 −12 for assembling each final cell bound in binary64. The endpoint derivative formulas are bounded in absolute value by two, the cell widths are at most one, and the assembly uses fewer than 50 arithmetic operations with intermediate absolute values below 16. With unit roundoff 2 −53 , the total absolute error, including the final subtraction, is below 10 −12 . The recorded pruning margin exceeds this allowance together with the error in the comparison target. Every cell is therefore below the exact rational target 58213 /1000000 = 0.058213, proving (H.2). Here nextafter(x,+infinity) returns the smallest binary64 number strictly greater than x. The acceptance threshold is nextafter(2e-12,+infinity) . A terminal cell is accepted only if its computed margin is finite and exceeds this threshold. This reserve is larger than 10 −12 + 9 × 10 −19 and thus covers both final assembly and target rounding. The executable checks the minimum margin once more before reporting success. Since the terminal cells cover the entire square, these checks establish the uniform inequality. 

H.4 Public source and reproduction 

The original source and certificate output supporting Proposition 13.2 are available from Du [27] in the GitHub repository 

https://github.com/Duke1968/dicut-gaussian-certificate .The source snapshot is identified by commit 

cbca544a2f248558f6303590214485f15562b7d4 .The same certificate files are included at anc/explicit_input_hardness/ : the C verifier, local MPFR header, exact-parameter script, verification output, and compilation and environment records. The SHA-256 digest of the SHA256SUMS manifest is 

16a92643ba25ccea23696027db0ab9289ce20648feb56a2fbf5a78c0a68ce561 .The manifest identifies the other nine files in that directory. The C verifier checks the Gaussian inequality using MPFR evaluation, the quadrature remainder, outward error allowances, and adaptive subdivision. In the C source and its output, L denotes 𝐻 𝐺 . The Python script checks the rational parameters and derived ratios in Appendix I. Theorem 12.6 supplies the separate reduction from the Gaussian inequality to computational hardness. The directory’s README provides the compilation and verification commands, together with the required arithmetic assumptions and software environment. 74 Concave roofs and downward recovery Donglei Du 

Reserve-aware source-package version. The directory anc/gaussian_reserve_v1/ contains the C source that enforces the reserve, its local header, a patch against the cited public source, and a separate manifest. The cited commit identifies the original verifier; the reserve-enforcing variant is included in this source package. Its output, compiler and environment information, and measured resources are recorded in

# I Exact parameter identities

Thus a diagonal shift can make the quadratic matrix positive semidefinite and be constant on the chosen Boolean encoding, yet still fail to preserve a multiplicative guarantee for the original objective. 

# K Outcome-space benchmark and prophet transfer 

The prophet consequence of Theorem 11.2 uses a valuation on the entire outcome universe. Its fractional benchmark must respect both outcome probabilities and item feasibility; we establish that benchmark and the transfer here. 

K.1 Finite outcomes and the fractional benchmark

Proposition K.1 (Outcome-space transfer) . Assume the valuation on all of 𝑈 has representation (11.1) . For

Proof. Put ¯ 𝑧 𝑖 = Í𝑒 ∈𝑈 𝑖 𝑧 𝑒 . Item activations are independent with marginals ¯ 𝑧 ∈ 𝑏𝑃 I . An equivalent procedure independently chooses a candidate outcome for each item with ¯ 𝑧 𝑖 > 0, using probabilities 𝑧 𝑒 /¯𝑧 𝑖 , and then 76 Concave roofs and downward recovery Donglei Du 

activates the candidate with probability ¯ 𝑧 𝑖 . Conditional on these candidates, apply Theorem 11.2. Restricting to the candidate set preserves (11.1): a cut becomes a cut, a coverage indicator, or zero, and unaries may become constants. Because the OCRS depends only on ¯ 𝑧 and is independent of the candidate identities, its selectability remains 𝑐 for every fixed candidate set. Let 𝐴 𝑧 be the set of activated candidates, which contains at most one outcome per item. Averaging the conditional OCRS inequalities gives E 𝑓 (𝑆 alg ) ≥ 𝑐 E 𝑓 ( 𝐴 𝑧 ). It remains to show E 𝑓 ( 𝐴 𝑧 ) ≥ 𝐹 (𝑧 ). Conditional on the other items, the submodular function ℎ on one item’s outcomes satisfies 

ℎ(𝑆 ) ≤ ℎ(∅) + ∑︁ 

> 𝑒 ∈𝑆

 ℎ({ 𝑒 }) − ℎ(∅) .

Taking an independent sample with marginals (𝑧 𝑒 )𝑒 ∈𝑈 𝑖 bounds its expectation by (1 − ¯𝑧 𝑖 )ℎ(∅) + Í𝑒 𝑧 𝑒 ℎ({ 𝑒 }) ,which is the expectation for that item under 𝐴 𝑧 . Replace the item blocks one at a time to prove this comparison. This is the submodular-dominance mechanism relevant here [49]. □

Corollary K.2 (Modular fractional-to-prophet guarantee) . Under Proposition K.1, assume the item-space OCRS is efficient and let 𝛼, 𝜀 ≥ 0. Any efficiently computed 𝑧 ∈ 𝑏𝑃 out satisfying 𝐹 (𝑧 ) ≥ 𝛼 OPT prop −𝜀 gives an online guarantee 

E 𝑓 (𝑆 alg ) ≥ 𝑐𝛼 OPT prop −𝑐𝜀. 

In particular, suppose the full outcome valuation also has an efficiently optimizable roof and a downward recovery inequality of factor 𝜌 . Then a 𝛿 -accurate roof optimizer gives 

E 𝑓 (𝑆 alg ) ≥ 𝑏𝑐𝜌 (OPT prop −𝛿 ). (K.3) 

Proof. The first assertion is Proposition K.1. For the second, optimize 𝐿 over (K.1), recover 𝑦 ≤ 𝑥 with 

𝐹 (𝑦 ) ≥ 𝜌𝐿 (𝑥 ), and set 𝑧 = 𝑏𝑦 . Then 𝑧 ∈ 𝑏𝑃 out . Since 𝑦 ≥ 0 and every entry of ∇2 𝐹 is nonpositive, 

𝑑 2 𝐹 (𝑡𝑦 )/ 𝑑𝑡 2 = 𝑦 ⊤∇2 𝐹 (𝑡𝑦 )𝑦 ≤ 0. Concavity and 𝐹 (0) ≥ 0 give 𝐹 (𝑏𝑦 ) ≥ 𝑏𝐹 (𝑦 ). Apply (K.2). □

For bit-polynomial implementations, the finite outcome lists and known rational probabilities are explicitly supplied with polynomially bounded encoding length. On 𝑈 , quadratic, cubic, and supplied bounded-degree rooted valuations have factors 𝜌 of 1 /2, 8 /17, and 8 /17 through degree twelve, respectively. Arbitrary finite rooted representations have factor 𝛾 ∗. For arbitrary arity-four factors, this online transfer additionally requires the global representation (11.1). 

# L Details of the finite-type hardness reduction 

We prove the invariance, decoding, and replication steps behind the affine criterion, using the definitions and hypotheses of Section 12. 

L.1 Finite-type invariance 

Proof of Lemma 12.4. For a used pair (𝑠, 𝑡 ), put 𝑌 ′ := 1 − 𝑌 and ℎ𝑡 (𝑦 ′) := 1 − 𝑔 𝑡 (1 − 𝑦 ′). Apply Mossel’s two-function theorem to 𝑔 𝑠 and ℎ𝑡 on the correlated space (𝑋, 𝑌 ′). By Lemma 12.3, its maximal correlation is −𝜌 𝑠𝑡 , so the Gaussian upper bound is 

Γ−𝜌 𝑠𝑡 (𝑚 𝑠 , 1 − 𝑚 𝑡 ) = 𝐷 𝜌 𝑠𝑡 (𝑚 𝑠 , 𝑚 𝑡 ).

The two-function refinement applies whenever no coordinate has large low-degree influence in both functions [43, Theorem 6.3 and Lemma 6.8, arXiv:math/0703683v6]; input and output complementation do not change influence. Choose the same 𝑑, 𝜏 for the finitely many used pairs and apply each pairwise bound with error 𝜂 .Multiplying by 𝑊 𝑠𝑡 and using Í𝑠,𝑡 𝑊 𝑠𝑡 = 1 proves (12.6). □

L.3 Affine finite-type reduction 

Proof of Theorem 12.6. Use the regular long-code construction with a global cardinality constraint [5, 50]. For every 𝑣 ∈ 𝑉 and 𝑠 ∈ T , create a type-𝑠 cloud indexed by 𝑥 ∈ { 0, 1}𝑅 with vertex mass 1

|𝑉 | 𝜋 𝑠 P𝑞 𝑠 [𝑥 ].

Sample an arc by choosing 𝑢 ∈ 𝑈 uniformly and ordered neighbors 𝑣, 𝑤 ∈ 𝑁 (𝑢 ) independently. Choose (𝑠, 𝑡 )

with probability 𝑊 𝑠𝑡 , draw (𝑋, 𝑌 ) ∼ 𝜈 ⊗𝑅 𝑠𝑡 , and apply the edge permutations. Place the arc from the type-𝑠 

cloud of 𝑣 to the type-𝑡 cloud of 𝑤 . For a cut with cloud indicators 𝑔 𝑣,𝑠 , its value is exactly the left-hand side of (12.8), with 𝑓 𝑢,𝑠 as in (12.7). Suppose a labeling satisfies at least 1 − 𝜁 of the Unique-Games edges. Select in each cloud the dictator coordinate given by its right-vertex label. The selected vertex mass is exactly 𝜇 . Both sampled constraints are satisfied with probability at least 1 − 2𝜁 ; conditional on this event the two pulled-back dictators use the same coordinate and a type-(𝑠, 𝑡 ) arc is cut with probability 𝜈 𝑠𝑡 (1, 0). Nonnegativity of arc weights gives completeness at least (1 − 2𝜁 )𝐶 .For soundness, fix 𝜂 > 0 and take 𝜅 from Lemma 12.5. Invoke the Unique Games Conjecture with no-case value at most 𝜅 /2, so that the decoding contrapositive applies. Equation (12.8) then bounds every cut by 

E𝑢 [Ψ(𝑚 𝑢 )] + 𝜂 . Regularity expresses its selected vertex mass as E𝑢 [𝜋 ⊤𝑚 𝑢 ]. A budget density at most 𝜇 + 𝑟 

and (12.9) therefore give cut value ≤ 𝛽 + 𝜆 (𝜇 + 𝑟 ) + 𝜂. 

Because all cloud probabilities are fixed rationals, replace a cloud vertex of rational mass by the corresponding number of equal-mass copies. Replace each original arc by the complete bipartite bundle between its two copy sets, distributing the arc weight by the product of the endpoint copy proportions. For 78 Concave roofs and downward recovery Donglei Du 

an arbitrary cut of the replicated instance, define 𝑔 𝑣,𝑠 (𝑥 ) ∈ [ 0, 1] as the selected fraction of the copies of (𝑣, 𝑠, 𝑥 ). By bilinearity, its cut value and selected-vertex fraction are given by the same expressions with these fractional indicators. Soundness therefore holds even for cuts that separate copies. For a fixed gap tolerance, the denominators of the type masses and product probabilities, the alphabet size 𝑅 , and the additional replication factor are constants. Both the number 𝑛 ′ of copies and the explicit rational arc list are consequently polynomial in the outer instance size. Multiplying all copy counts by a further integer 𝑀 

makes every copy set have at least 𝑀 vertices. In a bundle from a cloud vertex to itself, self-loops carry at most a 1 /𝑀 fraction of the bundle weight. They contribute zero to every cut, so delete them and normalize the remaining arc weight to one. If their total weight is ℓ ≤ 1/𝑀 , the soundness bound is divided by 1 − ℓ,while completeness cannot decrease. Taking 𝑘 = ⌈𝜇𝑛 ′⌉ gives 𝑘 /𝑛 ′ = 𝜇 + 𝑜 (1). Choose 𝜁 , 𝜂 , 1 /𝑀 , and the resulting density error 𝑟 sufficiently small relative to 𝛿 . The Unique Games Conjecture then yields the gap (12.10). If an approximation ratio exceeded (𝛽 + 𝜆𝜇 )/ 𝐶 by a fixed amount, choosing 𝛿 sufficiently small would make the algorithm’s output values distinguish the two cases. □

# Statements and declarations 

Funding. This work was supported by the Natural Sciences and Engineering Research Council of Canada, grant RGPIN/3840-2024. 

Competing interests. The author declares no relevant financial or nonfinancial competing interests. 

Code and proof certificates. The three GitHub repositories listed in Appendices C, D, and H.4 contain, respectively, the Lean formalization of Theorem 5.5, the exact-arithmetic certificates for Theorem 6.3, and the MPFR-based Gaussian certificate for Proposition 13.2. These appendices specify the source versions, certificate identifiers, and reproduction commands. The article’s ancillary material also includes the arity-four and Gaussian certificate files. The reserve-enforcing Gaussian verifier and its output are supplied alongside the separately identified public snapshot; Appendix H explains how they correspond. All remaining proofs appear in the manuscript. 

Generative AI assistance. ChatGPT assisted with exposition and LaTeX preparation, literature-source auditing, and supporting symbolic, exact-arithmetic, and proof-software checks. The author is responsible for providing the preliminary research notes that initiated the project, guiding multiple rounds of collaborative feedback. Additionally, the author maintains full accountability for the mathematical content and its alignment with the proof artifacts.
